# GLORY OF THE WORLD <note>original: "GLORIA MVNDI"</note>,
### Otherwise/
## The Table of Paradise:
->That is/<-

An exquisite <term>Book of Arts</term> <note>German: *Kunstbuch*; a common title for early modern collections of practical, alchemical, or technical recipes</note>, the likes of which nothing better has been experienced or obtained on earth, nor can anything better be invented: Which indicates the true sayings of the right <term>Philosophy</term> <note>In this context, "Philosophy" refers specifically to *Natural Philosophy* or Alchemy</note>, together with the very best and noblest Medicine and the highest <term>Tincture</term> <note>A potent medicinal or transformative substance, often synonymous with the Philosopher’s Stone</note>, along with many other proven arts and their necessary instruments.

Now I begin in the name of God the Almighty Creator and Redeemer, to fulfill the hidden mystery which was implanted in Nature by God, and given to the human race for knowledge, so that they may recognize how wondrously all things are created, and all species

## Another Understanding of Our Blessed Stone

The philosopher <note>Morienus Romanus was a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist, famously credited with bringing alchemical knowledge from the Christian world to the Arab prince Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> Morienus speaks and calls our **Stone** a **water**; in this, he does not write a lie. O you water in a sharp form <note>original: "sawrer gestalt"; while it translates literally as "sour," in an alchemical context it implies an acidic, corrosive, or penetrating quality necessary for transformation.</note>, you who sustain the **elements**! O you nature of proximity, you who dissolve nature! O you best of all natures, you who surpass nature! O you greatest of all natures, you who compose the purification! O you nature quite subtle <note>original: "schlim"; in Early New High German, this often meant smooth or sleek, referring here to the refined, non-viscous state of the alchemical matter.</note>, you who exalt nature! You are born and crowned with a **light**, and you who contain the four **elements** within yourself, and the <term>fifth essence <note>original: "fünffte wesen"; also known as the Quintessence, the purest and most potent form of matter that exists beyond the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, and water).</note></term> has sprung forth from you. In this simplicity, the simple-hearted rejoice: that nature has received the composition before the composition, unknown to the providence of men. Although you yourself give birth to the **mist**, and are indeed a **mother**, you require no help of any kind; therefore, nature contains nature, so that nature is not separated from the work of nature, and it is a simple thing.

<summary>This page presents a poetic and paradoxical invocation of the Philosopher's Stone, characterizing it as a "water" and a "mother" that contains all four elements and the quintessence.</summary>
<keywords>Morienus, Philosopher's Stone, Alchemical Water, Elements, Quintessence, Nature, Composition, Paradox</keywords>

### The interpretation follows.

### Interpretation

It dissolves <note>original: *solvirt*</note> and congeals <note>original: *coagulirt*</note> itself; that is, as the philosopher says, it opens and shuts itself. In that it congeals, it locks the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to the Great Work of alchemy.</note>; and in that it dissolves, it unlocks the Art. # Now I will write of its entrance / and will leave aside four things / and name it according to three things.

The Vegetable, Animal, and Mineral Stones <note>Alchemists categorized the primary matter of their work based on its origin: plants (vegetable), living creatures (animal), or earth/ores (mineral).</note>, and each one specifically / their effects / with many things / and I will now also leave aside these 3 things / and take for myself one thing / in which the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the vessel that catches the distilled liquid</note> / well-luted <note>sealed with a specialized alchemical paste or clay to make the joints airtight</note> / as one is accustomed to do / so do here as well / in all things that you wish to take / for the lovely Art / where it is found / in the same way as one finds it in the *Aqua fortis* <note>original: *Aqua fort.*; <meta>This page continues the practical chemical instructions following the preparation of the "Field" or base material. The **Body** <note>original: *Cörper*; the solid material or "dregs" remaining after the liquid has been distilled</note> remains at the bottom in the glass, within which there are still two elements that the **Fire** <note>The text uses the alchemical symbol <term>△</term> for Fire</note> cannot separate. The more one burns the ashes in the fire, the stronger the **Salt** <note>original: *Sal*; in alchemy, Salt represents the principle of fixity and the physical body</note> becomes. The **Earth** may be **calcined** <note>original: *Calcionirn*; a process of heating a substance to high temperatures in a crucible to drive off volatile matter and reduce it to a purified powder</note> until it becomes white; then it separates easily and unites gladly with its own earth, for everything presses toward its own kind. 

This earth thirsts so greatly for that which you have seen; know that it shall become alive. Give it a **cold, moist element** <note>Water, one of the four classical elements</note> to drink, so that the liquid rises a stalk's width above the earth, and then let it stand for eight days so that they may unite well with one another. Thus, you must be very mindful of how you are to proceed further. 

I have promised you no more than what I have told you thus far: **Sol** and **Luna** <note>Latin for "Sun" and "Moon," representing Gold and Silver, or the masculine and feminine principles of nature</note> must come together as **husband and wife**, otherwise the **Art** <note>The "Great Work" or *Magnum Opus* of alchemy</note> can never be perfected. # Now I will write of its beginning, and will leave behind four things, and name it according to three things.

### Another insight from Senior regarding Matter and Form.

The **Philosopher** <note>Refers to "Senior," the Latinized name of the 10th-century Arabian alchemist Zadith ben Hamuel, whose work was highly influential in the West</note> speaks thus: Natural things are those which have a natural origin, **Matter and Form** <note>original: *Materia vnd Forma*; the physical substance and the essential nature of a thing, a concept rooted in Aristotelian philosophy</note>, which are born and crafted by Nature. Just as a stone or a tree has **Matter and Form** through Nature, they are different from Nature itself; and what is composed of these two is called "natural." The nature of our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>, in its first stage, has come forth powerfully as **Lunaria** <term>Lunaria</term> <note>Literally "moon-like"; representing the silver, feminine, or "white" stage of the alchemical process</note> out of a **white coagulated earth** <note>original: *weissen Coagulirten Erden*; a purified solid state of the matter after it has been dissolved and re-hardened</note>. As the **Philosopher** says: place our **Sol** <note>Latin: "Sun"; representing the gold or masculine principle</note> into our white, **foliated earth** <note>original: *geblettert Erden*; referring to a flaky, leaf-like crystalline structure (Terra Foliata) often seen in purified salts</note>. But the instrument with which our **Art** is composed is **water** that has been **rectified** <term>rectified</term> <note>Purified through repeated distillation to increase its potency</note> two or three times through the **B. M.** <term>B. M.</term> <note>original: *Balneum Mariae*; a "Mary's Bath" or water bath, used to provide gentle, even heat for delicate distillations</note>. This is a vapor of an oily nature, and is the thing closest to our **Stone** and—

[one] could feel; the name of this was called <term>Hyle</term> <note>original: *Hyle*; a Greek term used in philosophy and alchemy to describe the most basic, formless primordial matter.</note>, that is, <term>First Matter</term> <note>original: *Prima Materia*; the fundamental substance from which all things in the universe were thought to be created.</note>, the beginning of all things. For when a thing is to be newly made out of nothing, then that same "nothing" must come together and become a single thing, and out of that single thing, a tangible <term>Matter</term> <note>original: *Materia*.</note> must arise, and out of that tangible Matter, a <term>Body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*.</note> must be formed, to which a living soul is given, whereby it gains its form through the grace of God, according to its kind.

Thus it is also to be noted: when God allowed the Matter to come into being, it consisted of dryness and moisture locked together. Now, if moisture were to grow from it, it had to be separated from the dryness—such as the fire for itself, and the earth also for itself. Likewise, the earth must be sprinkled with water if moisture is otherwise to grow from it, for without moisture, nothing can grow. **Hyle** <note>From the Greek word *hylē*, meaning wood or raw material; in alchemy, it refers to the primordial substance of the universe.</note> is the first matter, and the salt of the philosophers <term>Philosophorum</term>: the universal solvent <term>Azoth</term> <note>original: *Azoth*; a term for the "A to Z" or the essential mercury believed to be the transformative agent in alchemy.</note>, and the seed of all metals, which is drawn out of the body of Magnesia <term>Magnesiæ</term> and Lunaria <term>Lunariæ</term> <note>Likely referring to alchemical substances associated with the "white" or silver stage of the work, rather than the common minerals or plants we know today.</note>.

**Hyle** is the beginning of all things, and in the beginning it was a single matter <term>Materia</term>; this same substance was neither moist nor dry; it was neither earth nor water; it was neither light nor dark; neither air nor fire; but it was a tangled mixture <note>A description of the "Prima Materia" or primordial chaos before the elements were separated.</note>, which is **Hyle**.

<summary>This page concludes a warning against the misuse of alchemical knowledge and introduces "Hyle" as the primordial, undifferentiated substance from which all matter and metals originate.</summary>

<meta>This page marks a transition from the philosophical definition of "Hyle" to the second part of the book, which grounds the study of nature and alchemy in a biblical narrative of the Fall of Man. This provides a theological justification for the necessity of medicine and labor.</meta>

# The Second Part of this Book Follows.

First, it must be understood that when God Almighty, in the beginning, along with all heavenly and earthly bodies, placed our first father Adam into Paradise and forbade him from eating the fruit of the tree—the apple <note>While the Bible mentions "fruit," the identification as an apple is a long-standing Western tradition.</note>—under penalty of eternal death; at that same time, before Adam transgressed the commandment, man had no need for any thing on Earth, seeing as man was still immortal at that time. However, as soon as he had fallen by eating the forbidden fruit of the knowledge of good and evil, he immediately became needy of all things in the world, after he was driven by the angel, at God’s command, out of Paradise and into misery <note>original: *Elendt*; in Early New High German, this term specifically denotes "exile" or being "out of one's homeland," referring here to the expulsion from Eden into the world.</note>—that is, into this sorrowful world—finding nothing other than poverty, plague, and sick-

<summary>This page introduces the second part of the work, framing human suffering and the need for earthly resources as a direct consequence of Adam's exile from Paradise.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Paradise, the Fall, forbidden fruit, immortality, exile, human suffering, theology</keywords>

## Now Follows the Origin of the Metals.

Dear son, here I will indicate and show you the origin of the metals—from whence they have taken their origin and form—for thereafter you shall have to understand this thoroughly. And just as the metal arranges itself in every form and shape, so also does our Art <note>The "Art" refers to the practice of Alchemy.</note> arrange itself. But how that same process has a form and essence, I will explain and teach to you according to my meager understanding—as much as God the Lord has granted me grace—so that you, the reader, may all the sooner reach the foundation of the true Art.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 86 regarding how the positioning and purity of mountains influence the growth of metals, specifically how they are shaped by planetary forces.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<sig>F iiij</sig>

<note>original: *Planeten zulegen*; referring to the planets' influence or qualities being assigned to the mountains.</note> -the planets, the more the metals increase in their growth and receive their vapor <note>original: *Rauch*; literally smoke, but referring to the alchemical "fumes" or vapors that condense into ore.</note> and taste from them. For all metals have the property that they are eventually transformed into the Sun <note>original: *Sol*; the alchemical name for Gold.</note> and the Moon <note>original: *Luna*; the alchemical name for Silver.</note> through the long-lasting influence of the Sun and the Moon. For the Sun is the father of the metals—and indeed of all growing things—and the Moon is the mother. For you see that gold shines like the sun, and silver like the moon. For nothing more is lacking than the **element of fire**; of this they have too little, as you see, when the sun [faces] the

## What the Urine of Boys Is <note>original Latin: *Vrina puerorum quid sit*</note>

## The Saying of Abel.

2. Abel, the son of Adam, wrote in his beginning—from the time when God created our father Adam and placed him in Paradise—that He [God] cast into our father Adam the essence of all things, herbs, mountains, stones, and animals, called <term>tienit</term> <note>original: *tienit*. This is likely a corruption of "tincture" or a specific vital force that connects the physical and spiritual realms.</note>, so that he should be a heart of all things. From this, Nature brought it about that man should be—and may be called and considered—a mountain of all mountains, a stone of all stones, a tree of all trees, an herb of all herbs, a root of all roots, and an earth of all earths. For God has given this to him: that all the powers of all things are preserved within the human being.

3. Seth, the son of Adam, describes it to us thus: "You children, you shall know that however much vinegar <note>original: *Essig*. In alchemy, "Philosophic Vinegar" refers to a powerful acidic solvent used to break down substances at the start of the Great Work.</note> is boiled away in the Art and turned to ash, that is how many times it brings forth [its fruit] and makes the body <term>Corpus</term> <note>original: *Corpus*. The physical or solid matter being worked upon in the alchemical vessel.</note> white. If you seethe or boil it well and rob it of its blackness <note>In alchemy, the "blackness" or *nigredo* represents the initial stage of decay and purification; removing it is essential to progress to the white stage.</note>, then it transforms itself into the Stone."

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>This page explores the alchemical lineage from Adam to his sons Abel and Seth, emphasizing that man contains the essence of the entire world and describing the process of purifying matter into the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

## The Interpretation of the Saying of Seth, the Son of Adam.

The philosopher Seth <note>In alchemical tradition, Seth (the son of Adam) was often regarded as one of the first masters of the "Hermetic Art," who allegedly engraved the secrets of the world on stone pillars to survive the Deluge.</note> says: as much **vinegar** <note>original: "Essig"; in alchemy, "Philosophical Vinegar" is a common term for a powerful acidic solvent or the "mercurial water" used to dissolve metals.</note> as is boiled away and turned to **ashes**—by this he means that as much of the distilled water, which we call <term>sperm <note>original: "Sperma"; here referring to the "seed" or generative life-force of the matter, not biological fluid.</note></term>, as is turned into a <term>body <note>original: "Corpus"; the solid, physical base of the alchemical substance.</note></term> through the long boiling (which he calls ashes); just so much does it produce out of the blackness of the <term>body</term>, and washes it white. For the drawn-off water transforms itself by means of the long boiling into a <term>body</term>, and all sorts of things rise up during the work, and it drives away the stench and blackness from the **Earth**. And you should know: if the Earth were not there, the <term>spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"; the volatile, vaporous, or "breath-like" essence of a substance.</note></term> could not be <term>coagulated <note>original: "coagulirt"; the process of turning a liquid or gas into a solid or semi-solid state.</note></term>, for it has no <term>receptacle <note>original: "Receptacel"; a container or vessel.</note></term> in which it could be contained; nor can it be <term>coagulated</term> in any other thing

<summary>This page concludes a series of instructions on moral conduct before beginning an interpretation of an alchemical maxim attributed to Seth, the son of Adam. The text explains the process of "whitening" the alchemical body by boiling "distilled water" (the spirit) until it solidifies within the "earth" (the receptacle).</summary>

<keywords>Seth, Adam, vinegar, ashes, sperm, corpus, spirit, coagulation, distillation, purification, whitening</keywords>

--- Page 205 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from page 156, where the catchword "wer-" joins with "den" to form "werden" (to be or to become), completing the explanation of the alchemical Earth being washed white.</meta>

to be; for in its own <term>spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"; the volatile or vaporous essence of the substance.</note></term> it is purified and bound; therefore the <term>spirit</term> purifies its own <term>body <note>original: "Corpus"; the solid, physical base of the matter.</note></term>, as Seth the Philosopher <note>A legendary figure in alchemy, often identified as the third son of Adam, believed to possess antediluvian wisdom.</note> speaks; it makes the <term>body</term> white, and he speaks truly. Further the Philosopher says: if you boil it well and rob it of its blackness, it turns into a stone, and the white stone is called a <term>penny <note>original: "Pfenning"; here used as a metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone as a "universal currency" that can be traded for any blessing.</note></term>. By this the Philosopher means: one should boil it well, gently, and slowly, and not too hot; then with time it transforms into a <term>body</term> that remains stable in the fire, and is called a stone, for it is stable; and it receives a white color, the whitest of all, and is called a penny. And this is the reason: just as he who has the penny can get beer and bread for it, he gets for it whatever he desires: Health, Wisdom, long life, <term>gold <note>original: "Sol"; the sun, representing gold in alchemical shorthand.</note></term>, <term>silver <note>original: "Luna"; the moon, representing silver.</note></term>, pearls, and precious stones. Therefore it is rightly called a penny, and everything that one cannot get for other pennies and all the world's goods, one obtains with this penny.

<meta>This page follows the discussion of the "Bountifulness" of the Body and its transformation into Oil and Water. # Interpretation of the Sayings
### of Pythagoras.

The **Philosopher** <note>original: "Philosoph"; in this context referring to Pythagoras. Alchemical texts frequently cited ancient philosophers to lend authority to their chemical processes.</note> says: what is it that one feels, and does not see, and does not know? By this, the **Philosopher** means it is a **Matter** <note>original: "Ding"; literally "thing," but here referring to the *Materia Prima* or the "First Matter" of the alchemical work.</note> of our **Art** <note>original: "Kunst"; the "Art" is the standard term used by practitioners to describe the practice of Alchemy.</note>, which is prepared through our **Art**. And it is a **Matter** that one feels and does not see; this is to be understood thus: one feels it, but one does not see it, and one does not know its effect. 

But whoever knows it, yet does not know its effect, shall never arrive at the **Art**. For there is no more than one single **Matter** in the entire world from which the **Art** is made and worked. And this **Matter** comes from a dark place where it is never seen, and its effect and **Virtue** <note>original: "Tugent"; referring to the inherent power or medicinal/transformative potency of a substance.</note> are recognized by no one except the wise masters of this **Art**. Also, within the **Matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note>, the great **Mystery** <note>original: "Heimlichkeit"; literally "secrecy" or "hiddenness."</note> is hidden, such as the **Air** and the **Fire**—which is to say, the **Sun** <note>original: "Sol"</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"</note>.

<summary>This page provides an alchemical interpretation of a Pythagorean riddle, asserting that the entire Great Work relies on a single, hidden substance that is felt but not seen.</summary>
<keywords>Pythagoras, Alchemical Art, Materia Prima, First Matter, Sun and Moon, Sol and Luna, Elements, Hidden Mystery</keywords>

--- Page 218 ---

But that which one feels and does not see is the Earth. By this, the **Philosopher** means that the Earth is the **Density** <note>original: "Dickheit"</note> or **Body** <note>original: "Corpus"; the physical, solid aspect of the alchemical substance.</note> which is found at the bottom of the **Matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note>, which has gathered into a mass within the **Matter**; this one can feel and recognize.

Further, as he says: "therein is integrity and senses," such as smelling, tasting, hearing, and touching—by this, the **Philosopher** means that within Man there is integrity, namely the noble **Soul** <note>original: "Seel"</note> and the senses; for Man can recognize and contemplate these things, and can touch, feel, and understand them.

## Interpretation of the Saying of Aristeus <note>Aristeus is a legendary figure often cited in alchemical literature, particularly in the "Turba Philosophorum," as an authority on the transformation of matter.</note>

"Take the **Body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> that I have shown you, and make it into thin leaves." By this, the **Philosopher** <note>In this context, "the Philosopher" usually refers to the cited author, Aristeus, or generically to a master of the Art.</note> means that you should take the **Earth** <note>original: "Erdt"</note> which has settled to the bottom of our **Matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note> and has become dry. This **Earth** now reveals itself, and yet was never seen or recognized before; but now it is **Water** and **Earth**. This is what "shown" means: for the **Earth** has become manifest and is divided into two parts, namely into **Earth** and **Water**. 

The **Earth** should be taken and sealed in a glass and placed in the warm bath <note>Likely the "balneum Mariae" or water bath, a vessel of warm water used to provide gentle, even heat to a glass flask.</note>, so that in the heat or boiling it **dissolves** <note>original: "solvire"</note> into itself into **Water**. This is what the **Philosopher** means by "made into thin leaves." For the **Body** that has thus been formed is what the **Philosophers** call the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: "lapis Philosophorum"</note>, as the **Stone** of the...

# Interpretation of the Saying
## of Pythagoras.
<note>original: "Pythagoræ"</note>

You may well marvel at the strange and wondrous sayings of the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophen: the historical and legendary masters of alchemy whose writings form the basis of the tradition.</term>, and how in manifold ways they have described this **Thing** <note>This "Thing" refers to the Great Work or the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; yet they agree on one single point, and all their sayings emerge from one source. If you pay heed correctly, you will experience the truth, as the **Philosopher** <note>original: "Philosophus"; likely referring to an authority such as Hermes or Geber.</note> says: we give you a **Rule** <term>Regiment: a specific set of instructions or a governing procedure, often relating to the regulation of heat or the stages of the process.</term> for these things—that it be **Water**, dry as the **Water of the Moon** <note>original: "Wasser deß Mondes"; a metaphorical term for the "white" stage of the work (albedo) or a specific purified mercury.</note> which you have prepared. By this, the **Philosopher** means that when we wish to work in this **Art** <term>Kunst: the "Sacred Art" of alchemy.</term>, we must govern ourselves strictly according to the true understanding. If we then fail and do not hit the mark, we might rightly call them deceivers; for they provide the **Rule** openly. But if you do not succeed, it is only the fault of your own cleverness or folly, because you interpret the **Writing** <term>Schrifft: the authoritative alchemical texts and manuscripts.</term> according to your own mad heads, differently than they

# Interpretation and Meaning of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes

As Hermes then says: "The Art is true, and most certain of all." By this he means that the Art is correctly given by the **Philosophers**. However, the unwise interpret the writings differently than they read, and they take up things differently than they should. And when they then fail and do not succeed, they say the Art is not true and that the **Philosophers** have deceived them.

<summary>This page describes the transition from the white stage to the red stage through repeated cycles of dissolution and coagulation, and begins a commentary on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes, defending the truth of alchemy against those who misinterpret its cryptic instructions.</summary>

and write lies, and ruin the **Art** <term>Kunst: the practice of alchemy, often called the "Great Art" or "Royal Art."</term>, claiming that it is not true. <meta>This page continues the commentary on the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus from page 191. It elaborates on the maturation of the alchemical Stone and interprets the Hermetic precept regarding the power of the Stone when "turned into earth."</meta>

...and so it becomes a newborn fruit, in every way just as all fruits are generated in the mother’s womb; and as all creatures bear fruit, so also the Air or Wind bears our **Stone**, and not otherwise. Further, as Hermes says: "its power is complete when it is transformed into Earth." <note>original: *Vis eius integra est, si versa fuerit in terram*; a famous line from the Emerald Tablet signifying the "fixation" of the volatile spirit into a solid form.</note> By this he means that as soon as the Spirit <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile, gaseous principle</note> is transformed into a Body <note>original: *Corp.*, abbreviation for *Corpus*; the solid, physical form</note>, it then possesses its full powers. For you must understand that as long as the Spirit is still volatile <note>original: *flüchtig*</note> and not yet "fixed" or made stable, it works in that moment toward all that it is meant to achieve. To reach this point, one must handle it as the baker handles bread: for one takes a little of the Spirit and adds it to the Body, just as the baker adds the sourdough <note>original: *Saurtaig*; also called the leaven or ferment in alchemical practice</note> to the flour. The sourdough then transforms the entire substance into sourdough. Our Spirit—which is our sourdough—does the same; it transforms the entire substance, namely the Body <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, into its own substance. And so one must

# Another Little Treatise
### of the same content as the previous one, very useful to read.

->Preface <note>original Latin: *Præfatio*</note><-

Many people might well wonder why the philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> of this divine and most precious art—called the *Secret of the Philosophers* <note>original Latin: *Secretum Philosophorum*</note>—have devised so many hidden sayings in its honor, in order to hide the same from the unworthy; yet all of this creates nothing fruitful among the disciples of these secrets <term>Arcanen</term> <note>the term *Arcanen* (arcana) refers to the specialized, hidden knowledge or "mysteries" of alchemy that were kept from the general public</note>, because the philosophers have been intent on hiding the truth through such means. And when the ignorant read and hear such writings of theirs, and see what great riches 

<summary>This page introduces a new treatise on alchemy, opening with a preface that addresses the tradition of using cryptic language. The author explains that philosophers deliberately used "hidden sayings" to protect the divine art from the unworthy, even if it made learning difficult for new students.</summary>

<keywords>Another Little Treatise, Secretum Philosophorum, Arcanen, secrecy, alchemy, philosophers, preface, hidden knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 249 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface from the previous page. The word "thumb" at the beginning of the first line completes the word "Reichthumb" (riches) from the final sentence of page 198. The text continues to describe the pitfalls of greed and the necessity of divine grace in understanding alchemy.</meta>

riches, virtue, and power our art <term>Kunst</term> <note>The "Art" refers to the practice of alchemy, often called the "Royal Art" or "Divine Art" by its practitioners</note> can produce, it gives them a very sweet, lovely sound in their ears; so much so that they think they sit on golden chairs, and that the heavens hang full of stringed music. From this then springs a great zeal and desire, so that they think to quickly become a Doctor <note>In this context, "Doctor" refers to a learned master or someone who has reached the highest degree of knowledge in the art</note>, wishing to perform great miracles <note>original: *Miracul*</note>. Nor do they think that they might err, or that the philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> might deceive them.

But they do not know that the wise, from the very beginning, have hidden the foundation of this art, and have explained the truth only to their "children" <note>A common alchemical term for initiated students or worthy disciples</note> in parables <note>original: *Parabeln*</note> and allegories <note>original: *Gleichnussen*</note>. And it is impossible that a human being, as long as he may live in this sorrowful world, could fathom the sense and meaning of all philosophers—much less could he read through all their books and writings with true understanding. Thus, it is also even more impossible to obtain a firm foundation and the full fulfillment of one's desire from these same books and writings, unless it be that Almighty God <note>original: *Gott*</note>, out of special grace and mercy...

<summary>This page warns aspiring alchemists that the outward promise of wealth and miracles is a "sweet sound" that masks the difficulty of the art. It emphasizes that the philosophers' true meaning is hidden in parables and can only be understood through divine grace rather than just reading books.</summary>

Only the human **Adam** alone—**this is our Matter** <note>original: *Materia*; the base substance or "First Matter" that the alchemist begins with</note>—could not bring forth fruit by himself. And so that Nature might also fulfill its course in him, so that his race might be increased, the inner essence had to be separated from him and then rejoined to him as his **Eve**, as has been indicated before. 

This is to be understood in no other way than this: When our **Matter** <note>abbreviated as *Mat.* in the original</note> is in the beginning still coarse and whole, it cannot bring forth any fruit until it is divided, and the coarse is separated from the subtle, or the **Water** is separated from the **Earth**. The Water is reckoned as the **Eve** or **Spirit** <note>original: *Geist*</note>, and the Earth as the **Adam** or **Body** <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note>. This is not to be understood, however, as if the Spirit by itself alone becomes the Body, and the Body by itself alone becomes the Spirit; rather, they must be brought together, so that the **Spirit** <note>original Latin: *Spiritus*</note> as the water dissolves—that is, awakens—the **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> as the earth. And conversely, the Body accepts the Spirit as the water, and they unite with one another and become one **Mass** <note>original Latin: *Massa*; a unified, fermented substance</note>.

[white]ness is drawn out of the blackness, that is, out of the **Body** <note>the solid matter in the alchemical vessel</note>; and nothing more is needed here than patience and delay, as **Morienus** <note>a legendary 7th-century alchemist and hermit</note> speaks of it. This coagulated white water is the true **white foliated earth** <term>terra alba foliata: a stage in the alchemical process where the matter becomes white, pure, and flaky like leaves</term>, in which the **Philosopher** <note>a general term for an alchemical authority</note> tells us to sow our **Gold** and **Silver**, so that they may multiply with a hundredfold (1,000) fruit; and the clear **Fountain** that the **Count of Treviso** <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist famous for his allegorical "Parable of the Fountain"</note> makes mention of, into which the **King** enters to bathe himself. And when the King is sitting in the bath, none of his servants should come in to him; instead, they should keep the King’s clothes in safe-keeping until the King has dried out the entire fountain. Then the King will make all his servants into Lords, so that they will be as powerful in authority, power, and dominion—as well as strength and force—as the King himself was when he first entered the fountain. But the King is now adorned with threefold more dignity than he was before; for he wears a **threefold crown** <note>original Latin: *diadema*</note> upon his head, and wears a **ruby-red garment**, beneath which is the shirt of purity, girded with the band of justice. And thus he is a King of Life, rich in honor, whose dominion no person in this world can even imagine. Therefore, one should provide him with a pure, chaste **Bride**, who should be born from his own lineage, so that he may know <note>the text uses "erkennen," implying the biblical sense of intimate union</note> her and work within her, so that many noble children may be born of them both.

--- Page 284 ---
<meta>This page concludes the instruction on the transition between the primary colors of the Great Work—moving from the white stage (albedo) to the final red stage (rubedo)—and begins a new section attributed to Hermes Trismegistus concerning the philosophical foundations of the Art.</meta>

The redness is hidden and contained within the whiteness; one should not extract it <note>to "extract" here suggests trying to separate the colors prematurely or through aggressive chemical means</note>, but rather cook it gently until it becomes perfectly red. And regarding this whiteness, the **Assembly** <note>original: *Turba*, referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (The Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts</note> speaks thus: "When you seekers find the whiteness after the blackness, be certain that the redness follows the whiteness; for the redness is hidden within the whiteness. One must not extract it, but cook it gently until it becomes red." Herewith an end.

## Hermes Says.
<note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy and hermetic philosophy</note>

It is necessary to have knowledge of the beginning, both of natural things and of those made through the Art <note>original German: *Kunstreichen Dingen*; in this context, "the Art" refers specifically to the alchemical process</note>. For he who does not know the right beginning will also not reach the end.

### The Love of God and of one's Neighbor is the Perfection of all Wisdom.

To love God is the highest wisdom, and time is our possession. <note>original Latin: *Diligere Deum sapientia summa, & tempus possessio nostra*</note>

<vocab>redness, whiteness, Assembly, Hermes, beginning, end, love of God, wisdom</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the final transition of the alchemical matter from the white stage to the perfected red stage through patient heating. It introduces a new section by Hermes Trismegistus emphasizing that success in the Art depends on understanding its true beginnings and maintaining spiritual virtue.</summary>
<keywords>Rubedo, Albedo, Turba Philosophorum, Hermes Trismegistus, Alchemical Art, Beginning and End, Christian Wisdom, Alchemical Colors</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on the Albedo and Rubedo stages and the introductory wisdom of Hermes Trismegistus, this page presents the title page of the work, framing it as a revelation of nature's greatest secrets through both a "Chemical Kabbalistic Vision" and a defense of the true Philosopher's Stone against false preparations.</meta>

# THE OPENED CHEST OF THE MOST INGENIOUS
## SECRET <note>original Latin: *Aperta Arca Arcani Artificiosissimi*</note>

->That is:<-

# Opened and Open-
## Standing Chest of the greatest and most ingenious Secrets of Nature / of the Great and Small Farmer. <note>original: *Bawers* (Farmer); a common alchemical metaphor where the "Great Farmer" represents God or Nature, and the "Small Farmer" represents the alchemist cultivating the "seed" of the Stone</note>

### Alongside the right and true Round Natural Physics <note>original Latin: *Physica Naturali Rotunda*; "Round" here implies a complete, cyclical, or perfect system of natural laws</note>, described quite clearly through a Chemical Kabbalistic Vision:

->**And a**<-

### Warning, Instruction, and Proof / against all those / who falsely persuade others / that Potable Gold <note>original Latin: *Aurum potabile*; a legendary alchemical medicine consisting of gold dissolved into a drinkable liquid form</note> can be prepared by itself in a short time, outside of the Tincture of the universal Philosopher’s Stone <note>original Latin: *Lapidis philosophici*</note>.

### Shared out of Christian love with all hearts seeking the knowledge of natural things and the Philosopher’s Stone <note>original Latin: *Lapidem Philosophorum*</note> for the glory of God / and now published for the second time in a more correct edition.

---

### Published by Johan Carl Vnckel, Bookseller at Frankfurt am Main.
### 1623.

<summary>This is the title page for the "Aperta Arca," a famous alchemical text attributed to Johann Grasshoff, which promises to reveal the secrets of nature and the Philosopher's Stone. It emphasizes a "Round Natural Physics" and warns against fraudulent methods of creating medicinal gold without the proper alchemical foundation.</summary>
<keywords>Aperta Arca, Secrets of Nature, Great and Small Farmer, Round Natural Physics, Chemical Kabbalistic Vision, Potable Gold, Philosopher's Stone, Johan Carl Vnckel, Frankfurt am Main, 17th-century alchemy</keywords>
